I guess I just want to sleep a little better, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep at night, or is it mainly waking up early that's the issue?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess it's sort of both? I hope I'm explaining this right. Like I was saying earlier, when I first lay down, I have a hard time actually falling asleep because I'm just lying there, replaying the workday or, you know, little arguments from home. So it takes a while to finally drift off. \n\nBut then the other part is waking up at 3 or 4 AM and just... staying awake. By the time I have to get up and actually start the day, I feel like I never really rested at all. I know I shouldn't complain too much, because everyone gets tired, right? I'm sure it's just a normal phase. Does that... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Has this trouble with sleep been affecting your ability to function during the day, like making it harder to concentrate at work or causing irritability at home?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I wouldn't say it's affecting me *too* terribly. I just really want to get back to 100% so I'm not letting my team or my family down. Am I making sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these difficulties with sleep?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... I guess it's been a little while now. Maybe around a year and two months? Well, I mean, I'm still trying to stay active and involved. I really try not to let things slide, you know? But... I guess I have noticed a little bit of a shift.

So it takes a while to finally drift off. \n\nBut then the other part is waking up at 3 or 4 AM and just... staying awake. By the time I have to get up and actually start the day, I feel like I never really rested at all. I know I shouldn't complain too much, because everyone gets tired, right? I'm sure it's just a normal phase. Does that... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Has this trouble with sleep been affecting your ability to function during the day, like making it harder to concentrate at work or causing irritability at home?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I wouldn't say it's affecting me *too* terribly. I'm still showing up every day, still putting in the hours, you know? I don't want to sound like I'm dropping the ball completely. \n\nI guess... well, if I'm being completely honest, my team has been really great about stepping up lately. They've had to cover a few gaps for me when my sales forecasts or client prep kind of... slipped a little bit. Just minor things, really! And at home, I suppose I might be a little less present with my wife and the kids than I used to be. I just get so drained, you know? But I'm still trying my best to keep everything together. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad manager or a bad husband. Is it normal to struggle a little bit like that when you're not sleeping well?\n\nDoctor: Even if you manage to get several hours of sleep, do you still wake up feeling unrefreshed or notice that the sleep doesn't really restore your energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. Well, um... I really wish I could say it only happens when I'm completely exhausted. That would make a lot of sense, right? But... if I'm being completely honest, it's pretty much most days.

Well, I mean, I'm definitely still getting all my work done. I'd never let my team or our clients down, I promise. But, um... I guess if I'm really thinking about it, I have noticed that things are taking me just a little bit longer than they used to? 

Like, I used to be able to just knock out these sales forecasts and client decks pretty quickly. It was just second nature to me. But lately... I don't know, I'll find myself staring at the same slide and reading it, like, five times. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really try to stay active and keep up with everything, I do. I know it's important to have hobbies and be social, especially for my wife, so she doesn't worry. But... I guess, if I'm being totally honest, it has been a little harder lately to get excited about things. 

Like, I used to always look forward to my pickup basketball games, or going out for bowling nights with the guys. And we'd do music festivals, or I'd fire up the smoker in the backyard and have friends over. I was always the one organizing that stuff. But recently... I don't know, I find myself making excuses to cancel, or if I do go, I'm just kind of... going through the motions, you know? I'm there, and I'm smiling, but I'm not really feeling it. 

I feel terrible admitting that, because I don't want to seem ungrateful for my friends or my life. I really am trying to just snap out of it and enjoy things again. Is it... is it bad that I'm feeling that way about stuff I usually love? I hope I'm not sounding too negative.

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I think... I mean, I think I kind of touched on this a little bit earlier, didn't I? About the basketball and the bowling? 

I really, really don't want to sound like I'm just complaining about my life, because I have a great life. I do. But yeah, even if I'm not exhausted, it's just... it's hard to find that spark right now. I still go out there and give it my best effort, I promise. I'm not just sitting around moping all weekend. I just... I don't get that same feeling of satisfaction from it that I used to.

But then I end up waking up around 3 or 4 in the morning, and I can't really get back to sleep. When I finally get up for the day, I just feel like I haven't rested at all. Is that... does that make sense to come in for something like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot during the day, or feeling anxious about things even when you're not trying to fall asleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, not really. During the day, I feel like I handle things okay. Being a sales manager, you kind of have to keep a good face on and just keep moving, right? So I wouldn't say I'm anxious or worrying a lot while I'm at the office. \n\nIt's really just when everything gets quiet at night that my brain decides to start working overtime. I hope I'm explaining that right... I definitely don't want to make it sound like I can't handle my job or anything, because I can, and I usually keep a pretty positive attitude. I guess I just want to sleep a little better, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep at night, or is it mainly waking up early that's the issue?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess it's sort of both? I hope I'm explaining this right. Like I was saying earlier, when I first lay down, I have a hard time actually falling asleep because I'm just lying there, replaying the workday or, you know, little arguments from home. So it takes a while to finally drift off. \n\nBut then the other part is waking up at 3 or 4 AM and just... staying awake. By the time I have to get up and actually start the day, I feel like I never really rested at all. I know I shouldn't complain too much, because everyone gets tired, right? I'm sure it's just a normal phase. Does that... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Has this trouble with sleep been affecting your ability to function during the day, like making it harder to concentrate at work or causing irritability at home?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I wouldn't say it's affecting me *too* terribly. I'm still showing up every day, still putting in the hours, you know? I don't want to sound like I'm dropping the ball completely. \n\nI guess... well, if I'm being completely honest, my team has been really great about stepping up lately. They've had to cover a few gaps for me when my sales forecasts or client prep kind of... slipped a little bit. Just minor things, really! And at home, I suppose I might be a little less present with my wife and the kids than I used to be. I just get so drained, you know? But I'm still trying my best to keep everything together. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad manager or a bad husband. Is it normal to struggle a little bit like that when you're not sleeping well?\n\nDoctor: Even if you manage to get several hours of sleep, do you still wake up feeling unrefreshed or notice that the sleep doesn't really restore your energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. I'll lie in bed and kind of... replay things from the office, or maybe some minor disagreements at home. Nothing serious, just regular life stuff. Is that... does that make sense to come in for something like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot during the day, or feeling anxious about things even when you're not trying to fall asleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, not really. During the day, I feel like I handle things okay. Being a sales manager, you kind of have to keep a good face on and just keep moving, right? So I wouldn't say I'm anxious or worrying a lot while I'm at the office. \n\nIt's really just when everything gets quiet at night that my brain decides to start working overtime. I hope I'm explaining that right... I definitely don't want to make it sound like I can't handle my job or anything, because I can, and I usually keep a pretty positive attitude. I guess I just want to sleep a little better, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep at night, or is it mainly waking up early that's the issue?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess it's sort of both? I hope I'm explaining this right. Like I was saying earlier, when I first lay down, I have a hard time actually falling asleep because I'm just lying there, replaying the workday or, you know, little arguments from home. So it takes a while to finally drift off. \n\nBut then the other part is waking up at 3 or 4 AM and just... staying awake. By the time I have to get up and actually start the day, I feel like I never really rested at all. I know I shouldn't complain too much, because everyone gets tired, right? I'm sure it's just a normal phase. Does that... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Has this trouble with sleep been affecting your ability to function during the day, like making it harder to concentrate at work or causing irritability at home?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I wouldn't say it's affecting me *too* terribly. I'm still showing up every day, still putting in the hours, you know? I don't want to sound like I'm dropping the ball completely. \n\nI guess... well, if I'm being completely honest, my team has been really great about stepping up lately. They've had to cover a few gaps for me when my sales forecasts or client prep kind of... slipped a little bit. Just minor things, really! And at home, I suppose I might be a little less present with my wife and the kids than I used to be. I just get so drained, you know? But I'm still trying my best to keep everything together. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad manager or a bad husband. Is it normal to struggle a little bit like that when you're not sleeping well?\n\nDoctor: Even if you manage to get several hours of sleep, do you still wake up feeling unrefreshed or notice that the sleep doesn't really restore your energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. Even if I do manage to string a few hours together, I still start the day feeling like I never really rested at all. \n\nUm... I guess I should probably mention, that's actually what finally made me call the employee assistance program at work. I really didn't want to make a big fuss or anything, but because I’m barely sleeping right—between lying there replaying everything and then the 3 or 4 AM wake-ups—and just feeling so completely drained every morning... I figured I should probably reach out before it turns into a bigger issue. \n\nI hope it was okay for me to use that program for something like this? I just really want to get back to 100% so I'm not letting my team or my family down. Am I making sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these difficulties with sleep?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... I guess it's been a little while now. Maybe around a year and two months? Give or take? \n\nI really hope I should have come in sooner, I didn't want to waste anyone's time if it was just a phase. But yeah, it started around then. That was when, um, the arguments at home with my wife started getting a lot more frequent, and just... worse, I guess. It's nothing we can't handle, I promise! Every couple goes through rough patches, right? But I think that's when the sleep stuff really started to stick around. Does that timeframe sound about right for something like this?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood or motivation during the day, like feeling down, irritable, or losing interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I wouldn't say I'm completely miserable or anything like that. I'm still smiling for the clients and trying to be upbeat for the kids. But... I guess, if I really think about it, there's just this sort of... heaviness? \n\nLike, most days it just feels like this gray cloud kind of follows me around. From the office, to the car, and then back into the house. Even when things are perfectly fine and nothing bad is actually happening, I just feel a little... hollow, maybe? I don't want to sound ungrateful, because I know I have a good life and a great job. I just... I can't seem to shake that feeling lately. Is it normal to feel that way when you're just really tired all the time? I'm hoping it's just the lack of sleep catching up with me, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you once looked forward to?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I'm still trying to stay active and involved. I really try not to let things slide, you know? But... I guess I have noticed a little bit of a shift. \n\nLike, I used to always be the one organizing pickup basketball on the weekends, or getting the guys together for bowling nights. And I loved going to music festivals, or just firing up the smoker in the backyard and having friends over. I still think those things are great! It's just... lately, I find myself looking for excuses to cancel. Or if I do push myself to go, so I don't let anyone down, I just kind of feel like I'm... I don't know, just going through the motions? Like I'm there, but I'm not really *there*. \n\nI'm sure it's just because I'm so exhausted from not sleeping, right? I mean, it's hard to be the life of the party when you're running on empty. Does that make sense? I'm really hoping that once we get my sleep sorted out, I'll be right back to my old self.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of heaviness or hollowness been present most days, even when you’ve had a better night’s sleep, or does it seem to come and go only when you’re really tired?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really wish I could say it only happens when I'm completely exhausted. That would make a lot of sense, right? But... if I'm being completely honest, it's pretty much most days. \n\nLike I was saying earlier about that heavy, gray mood following me from the office, to the car, and into the house... it's just kind of always there now. Even on the rare nights where I actually manage to get a decent few hours of sleep, and even when absolutely nothing specific is going wrong in my day, I still just feel kind of... hollow inside. \n\nI really hope I'm not sounding like a complainer, because I know I should just be pushing through it, and I really do try my best to leave it at the door for my wife and my sales team, I promise. I don't want to bring anyone else down. Is it... is it normal that I'm feeling this way even on the days I sleep a little better? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel a lack of energy or feel fatigued almost every day, even when you haven't been doing anything particularly physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, obviously I'm tired because I haven't been sleeping well, like I mentioned. But if I'm not doing anything particularly hard... no, I don't just sit around feeling physically wiped out for no reason. \n\nI really try to keep my energy up, especially for the guys at work, you know? So, if I'm just having a quiet weekend or something, I still have the energy to get up and do what needs to be done. I'm definitely not just dragging all day every day if I haven't been working hard. I guess my energy levels are actually pretty okay, considering everything? \n\nI hope that's helpful... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of what's going on. Does that answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to focus, make decisions, or think clearly during the day, even when you haven’t been especially tired?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I'm definitely still getting all my work done. I'd never let my team or our clients down, I promise. But, um... I guess if I'm really thinking about it, I have noticed that things are taking me just a little bit longer than they used to? \n\nLike, I used to be able to just knock out these sales forecasts and client decks pretty quickly. It was just second nature to me. But lately... I don't know, I'll find myself staring at the same slide and reading it, like, five times. And then I start second-guessing every single number I put in there, and I just can't seem to decide what to actually say. \n\nI'm sure it's just a temporary rut, and I'm still pushing through it to get the work finished. I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm incompetent or slipping at my job, because I'm working really hard to make sure nobody at the office notices. I just... I guess my focus is just a little off right now. Does that make sense? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced days where nothing really seems enjoyable, even things you used to like, or where you just can't feel interested in much of anything?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to stay active and keep up with everything, I do. I know it's important to have hobbies and be social, especially for my wife, so she doesn't worry. But... I guess, if I'm being totally honest, it has been a little harder lately to get excited about things. \n\nLike, I used to always look forward to my pickup basketball games, or going out for bowling nights with the guys. And we'd do music festivals, or I'd fire up the smoker in the backyard and have friends over. I was always the one organizing that stuff. But recently... I don't know, I find myself making excuses to cancel, or if I do go, I'm just kind of... going through the motions, you know? I'm there, and I'm smiling, but I'm not really feeling it. \n\nI feel terrible admitting that, because I don't want to seem ungrateful for my friends or my life. I really am trying to just snap out of it and enjoy things again. Is it... is it bad that I'm feeling that way about stuff I usually love? I hope I'm not sounding too negative.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up at 3 or 4 AM, do you find that you can't fall back asleep at all, or do you sometimes fall back asleep but still feel unrested in the morning?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to just close my eyes and breathe, you know? I don't want to wake my wife up or be a bother. But yeah, when I wake up around 3 or 4, I usually can't get back to sleep at all. \n\nI just end up lying there, and my brain starts going over everything... like, replaying conversations with clients, or little arguments I've had at home. I try really hard to shut it off, but I just can't seem to. And then by the time I actually have to get up, I just feel like I haven't really rested at all. \n\nHonestly, that's kind of what pushed me to finally call the employee assistance program. I just want to make sure I'm well-rested so I can be at my best for my team and my family. I hope it's okay that I reached out to them... I just figured it was the responsible thing to do. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you no longer feel any pleasure or satisfaction from activities you used to really enjoy, even when you're not tired?\n\nPatient: Well, I think... I mean, I think I kind of touched on this a little bit earlier, didn't I? About the basketball and the bowling? \n\nI really, really don't want to sound like I'm just complaining about my life, because I have a great life. I do. But yeah, even if I'm not exhausted, it's just... it's hard to find that spark right now. I still go out there and give it my best effort, I promise. I'm not just sitting around moping all weekend. I just... I don't get that same feeling of satisfaction from it that I used to. \n\nI'm sure it's just a phase, though. If I just keep putting myself out there, it'll come back, right? I'm doing the right thing by trying to stay involved, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Even when you're not particularly tired, do you find that activities you used to enjoy no longer feel rewarding or feel like they used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, I'm sorry, did I... did I not explain that right a second ago? I really want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly, so please tell me if I'm not making sense. \n\nBut um, yeah, it's exactly like I was just saying with the basketball and the cookouts. Even if I'm wide awake and not tired at all, I just... I don't feel that same reward from them right now. I don't really have any other examples to give you, if that's what you mean? It's just those normal things I've always done. \n\nI really hope I'm giving you the information you need. Did I misunderstand the question, or is that what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve lost interest or don’t get pleasure from almost all activities, even the ones you usually enjoy, not just the specific ones you mentioned like basketball or cookouts?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I see what you're asking now. I'm sorry, I guess I was focusing too much on just those couple of things. I really want to be helpful here. \n\nTo answer your question... yeah, I suppose it's pretty much everything right now. Like, I used to really look forward to going to music festivals in the summer, or, you know, just firing up the smoker and having people over for a big barbecue. I love doing that for my friends. But lately... I don't know, I either end up finding a reason to cancel, or if I do go, I just feel like I'm going through the motions. I'm still smiling and making sure everyone else is having a good time, of course. I'd never want to ruin anyone else's day. \n\nIt's just... internally, I'm not really feeling that same joy from it. Is that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm explaining it right this time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around complaining, because honestly, I know I have a good life and things are fine. But... yes, I suppose I do. 

To answer your question, most days it feels like this... this heavy, sort of gray mood just kind of follows me around. Like, from the office, to the car, and then into the house. Even when absolutely nothing specific is happening or going wrong, I just feel kind of... hollow, I guess? 

I mean, I'm still getting my work done and managing my team, I promise I'm not letting it ruin anything or letting anyone down. I push through it. But internally, it's just kind of there in the background all day. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining that the right way.
